Ebola in Nigeria: No suspected case for northeast afta laboratory tests show negative - NCDC - BBC

News Brief
The Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC) confirmed that laboratory tests on two suspected Ebola cases in Adamawa state, Nigeria, have returned negative results, indicating no positive cases. The suspected cases were identified in Yola, the capital of the northeast region, and were isolated for further investigation. The NCDC emphasized that these are not confirmed cases yet and that the public should remain vigilant, practicing good hygiene and seeking immediate medical attention if symptoms like fever, weakness, or bleeding appear. The agency also noted that the two individuals were part of a broader effort to manage potential outbreaks, following the evacuation of an Ebola-positive patient from Nigeria to Switzerland. The results were released on 31 October 2026, after initial reports of suspected cases on 27 October 2026. The NCDC reiterated that the only confirmed case in Nigeria remains in the Federal Capital Territory (FCT).

"Anyone wey dey experience symptoms such as fever, severe weakness, headache, muscle pain, vomiting, diarrhoea or unexplained bleeding, particularly following relevant exposure, suppose seek immediate medical attention and inform healthcare workers of any relevant travel or contact history, NCDC say for dia statement. On Saturday, according to di Nigeria Center for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), dem say dis two new suspected cases na for di Northern region of di kontri. Dis come afta Nigeria evacuate di Ebola patient wey test positive for Abuja back to Switzerland, along wit her husband. Last week, for statement on dia official website, Swiss authorities say di woman dey receive treatment for Geneva University Hospital, while her husband dey for anoda separate facility. Di couple wey both of dem na Swiss nationals, dey repatriated from Abuja, Nigeria, on medical evacuation flight on Monday 25 October, 2026. Meanwhile, dis new suspected cases of Ebola for Nigeria no be confirmed cases until dem do laboratory test. Di agency say both patients don dey isolated, while dem don begin contact identification and also dey do lab test to establish weda di two don contract di virus. "Two suspected cases of Ebola disease dey identified for Nigeria North-East region. Both individuals don dey isolated, contact identification and listing dey ongoing, and laboratory testing dey conducted," NCDC tok for dia statement on Saturday 31 October, 2026. According to NCDC, di classified suspected cases no mean say dem be 'confirmed Ebola cases; confirmation dey subject to laboratory results.' "Di public dey advised to maintain good hand hygiene, practise enhanced sanitation and follow guidance from public health authorities," di authorities tok. Dem say dem go provide more details wen more korrect informate dey available. Dis na afta Nigeria govment don release ₦10bn ($6.7m) in emergency funding to support laboratory testing, purchase protective equipment, mobilise health workers and strengthen oda containment measures, afta di kontri confam one case of Ebola virus. Signs and symptoms of Ebola Ebola na rare but deadly disease wey virus dey cause. Ebola viruses normally dey infect animals, typically fruit bats, but outbreaks among humans dey sometimes start wen pipo eat or handle infected animals. E dey take two to 21 days for symptoms to appear. E dey come suddenly and start like flu or malaria, wit fever, headache and tiredness. Di symptoms include: Sudden high fever: Fever Aches and pains for di muscles and joints Serious headache Weakness and fatigue Sore throat Loss of appetite As di disease progress, di pesin go start to dey vomit and develop diarrhoea and e fit lead to organ failure. Some, no be all, patients dey develop internal and external bleeding. Di virus dey spread from one pesin to anoda by contact wit infected bodily fluids like blood or vomit. How to prevent Ebola virus Di Ebola outbreak on record na for 2014 for West Africa wit ova 11,000 pipo wey die. Dat epidemic kill five times more dan all oda known Ebola outbreaks wey don happun. Di current Ebola outbreak for di Democratic Republic of Congo now na di deadliest of di 17 outbreaks di kontri don face and e don continue to spread. Now, how you fit keep yourself safe and prevent di virus: - Wash your hands evri time wit soap and water - Health pipo say make we wash hands wit soap and water and clean surfaces wit chlorinated water. - No dey touch pipo wey don dey infected - To prevent more of di infection, health professionals advise make pipo avoid contact wit pipo wey dey infected, including for dem to stop to shake hands. - Avoid dead bodies. - No dey kill, touch or chop bush meat. E get vaccine for di Zaire strain of Ebola, wey dey common for di past for Democratic Republic of Congo. And currently no approved vaccine dey for di Sudan strain of Ebola. Howeva, early treatment of specific symptoms, fit improve survival of di pesin wey get am. For medical teams, dem must wear full Personal Protective Equipment, PPE, wen dem dey attend to cases to prevent infection. Deadi bodi dem, for any body bag, must dey buried by pipo wey dey wear proper PPE. Why Ebola dey always come back Ebola dey quick spread. Small sweat or spit from pesin or animal wey get di disease fit infect pesin. According to di WHO, new Ebola outbreak go ever dey happun for Dr Congo because di virus dey live for animal reservoir wey dey for many parts of di kontri. Fruit bats na di main carrier of di disease, but pipo fit dey infected if dem come in close contact wit blood, organs or bodily fluids of animals wey get di virus like chimpanzees, gorillas, monkeys, antelope and porcupines. Di disease dey very rampant for dis area and e no go dey possible to destroy all di animals wey fit dey carry Ebola. As long as pipo dey touch and eat dem, possibility dey say Ebola go always return. B routine priorityPublic-health news

HEALTH ALERT: Israel Isolates Two Feverish Travelers As Labs Test For Ebola

News Brief
On November 3, 2026, Israel’s health system activated an alert after two travelers returning from the Democratic Republic of Congo were isolated due to suspected Ebola virus infection. The Health Ministry confirmed that no confirmed cases have been diagnosed yet, and the individuals are undergoing laboratory tests to confirm or rule out the infection. Both patients were taken to designated medical centers—Sheba Medical Center in Tel Hashomer and Ichilov Medical Center in Tel Aviv—for treatment and monitoring under strict isolation protocols. Health officials are tracing all contacts of the two individuals as part of an epidemiological investigation. The ministry emphasized that Ebola spreads only through direct contact with bodily fluids or contaminated objects. Travelers from the Democratic Republic of Congo within the past 21 days who develop fever or unusual symptoms are advised to stay home and avoid contact with others. The situation remains under investigation, with no confirmed cases or deaths reported at this time.

(AP Photo/Sebastien Kitsa Musayi) Israel’s health system went on alert on Tuesday after two people who recently returned from the Democratic Republic of Congo came down with fever and were placed in isolation on suspicion that they have contracted the Ebola virus. The Health Ministry stressed that the cases are only suspected at this stage, and that no confirmed case of Ebola has been diagnosed in Israel. The two met the initial criteria for testing, and a series of laboratory tests is underway in line with standard protocols to confirm or rule out infection. Under the strict procedures for handling high-risk contagious diseases, the two were immediately taken for treatment and monitoring under tight isolation. One was brought to Sheba Medical Center at Tel Hashomer and the other to Ichilov Medical Center in Tel Aviv. Both hospitals were designated in advance to receive cases of this kind. At the same time, Health Ministry officials opened a wide epidemiological investigation to trace everyone who came into contact with the two patients. The ministry reminded the public that Ebola spreads only through direct contact with the bodily fluids or secretions of a sick person, or with contaminated objects. Travelers who have returned from Congo within the past 21 days and develop a fever above 38 degrees Celsius (100.4 Fahrenheit) or other unusual symptoms are told to stay home, avoid contact with others and contact the ministry right away, rather than going to a clinic or hospital without coordinating first. Against the backdrop of the ongoing outbreak in Africa, the ministry also issued a detailed travel warning. Israelis are urged to stay away entirely from the affected eastern and northern provinces of Congo and to avoid nonessential visits to the rest of the country. The ministry also advised reconsidering unnecessary trips to several of Congo’s neighbors, and urged extra caution for travelers heading to Kenya and Nigeria. 刚果有超过 5,000 例埃博拉死亡,肯尼亚和尼日利亚出现病例——为什么 疫苗 不能用于 Bundibugyo 毒株

Over 5,000 Ebola deaths in Congo, cases in Kenya and Nigeria—Why vaccines cannot be used for the Bundibugyo strain
新闻简报刚果民主共和国报告了由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒暴发,截至2026年10月31日,已有超过5,000人死亡,10,351人感染。肯尼亚和尼日利亚也有确诊病例,其中肯尼亚共9例,死亡1例,尼日利亚1例。暴发于2026年5月15日宣布,已蔓延至刚果8个省和66个卫生区。卫生当局正在监测接触者并调查感染源。截至2026年10月28日,世界卫生组织报告了10,274例确诊病例和4,992例死亡,而美联社援引10月31日的数据称,有10,351例感染和5,026例死亡。截至10月20日,确诊病例的粗病死率为48.3%。暴发并没有导致在最初的集群之外形成广泛的社区传播。Bundibugyo 菌株与 埃博拉病毒 不同,现有的 疫苗 未获批准用于该菌株。世界卫生组织和刚果卫生当局有不同的汇总方法和更新时间表,因此在比较数据时考虑这些差异非常重要。截至10月28日,日本尚未被列入世界卫生组织名单,该国境内尚未出现确诊病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
刚果的暴发于2026年5月15日宣布,更新数据于2026年10月20日、10月28日和10月31日。新闻报道的发布日期为2026年11月2日。
地点与对象
暴发 主要出现在刚果民主共和国,肯尼亚和尼日利亚也有确诊病例。受影响人群包括刚果8个省和66个卫生区的个人,以及肯尼亚和尼日利亚的接触者。
事件
刚果民主共和国报告了由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发,肯尼亚和尼日利亚也确诊了病例。该事件已得到卫生当局和世界卫生组织的确认,并提供了有关菌株和 疫苗 限制的具体细节。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月31日,刚果已有10,351人感染,5,026人死亡。截至 2026 年 10 月 28 日,世界卫生组织报告了 10,274 例确诊病例,4,992 例死亡。截至 2026 年 10 月 20 日,确诊病例的粗病死率为 48.3%。据世界卫生组织报告,暴发 并未导致在最初的聚集性感染群之外出现广泛的社区 传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生当局正在识别和监测接触者,详细调查正在进行中。世界卫生组织和刚果卫生当局正在提供最新数据,但汇总方法和更新时间存在差异。该数据基于确诊病例,不包括疑似病例。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Over 5,000 Ebola deaths in Congo, cases in Kenya and Nigeria—Why vaccines cannot be used for the Bundibugyo strain

News Brief
An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus has been reported in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, with over 5,000 deaths and 10,351 infections as of October 31, 2026. Cases have also been confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria, with a total of 9 cases and 1 death in Kenya and 1 case in Nigeria. The outbreak was declared on May 15, 2026, and has spread to 8 provinces and 66 health zones in Congo. Health authorities are monitoring contacts and investigating the source of infection. The WHO reported 10,274 confirmed cases and 4,992 deaths as of October 28, 2026, while the Associated Press cited 10,351 infections and 5,026 deaths on October 31. The crude case fatality rate among confirmed cases was 48.3% as of October 20. The outbreak has not led to widespread community transmission outside the initial cluster. The Bundibugyo strain is different from the Ebola virus, and existing vaccines are not approved for this strain. The WHO and Congolese health authorities have different aggregation methods and update timelines, so it is important to consider these differences when comparing figures. Japan is not included in the WHO list as of October 28, and no cases have been confirmed within the country.
Original source text (Japanese)
メインコンテンツへスキップ Over 5,000 Ebola deaths in Congo, cases in Kenya and Nigeria—Why vaccines cannot be used for the Bundibugyo strain SGNewsJAPAN Nov 2, 2026 06:25 Over 5,000 Ebola deaths in Congo, cases in Kenya and Nigeria—Why vaccines cannot be used for the Bundibugyo strain Table of Contents ◆ Ebola deaths in Congo exceed 5,000 ● 10,351 infections and 5,026 deaths reported on October 31 ● WHO tally shows 4,992 deaths as of October 28 ● 9,704 confirmed cases as of the WHO's October 20 announcement ◆ What is the Bundibugyo Ebola strain? ● Ebola is not a term that refers to only one virus ● Even if the virus strain is different, immune response is not necessarily the same ◆ Cases confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria ● Kenya confirms imported case and infection of contacts ● One imported case in Nigeria すべて表示 The Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak that began in the Democratic Republic of the Congo in May 2026 has seen an increase in infections and deaths within the country, with cases also confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria. "If there is a vaccine for Ebola, why have deaths exceeded 5,000?" The key to answering this question lies in the fact that there are multiple strains of the Ebola virus, existing vaccines are not approved for this specific strain, and early detection and treatment are being hindered by conflict and the collapse of medical systems. We will organize the confirmed facts and current challenges without spreading anxiety as if an epidemic has begun in Japan. ◆ Ebola deaths in Congo exceed 5,000 ● 10,351 infections and 5,026 deaths reported on October 31 On October 31, 2026, the Associated Press reported 10,351 infections and 5,026 deaths, citing the latest data provided by health authorities in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. The figure of over 5,000 deaths indicates the severity of the ongoing outbreak in the country. However, this report is based on data from health authorities within Congo and is not necessarily the same as the figures or reporting period used by the WHO. Because infectious disease statistics take time to confirm test results and aggregate reports, it is necessary to look at "when, who, and what scope was aggregated" when comparing numbers. ● WHO tally shows 4,992 deaths as of October 28 In the latest WHO dashboard, confirmed cases in Congo are listed as 10,274, with 4,992 confirmed deaths. This data is up to October 28, which is an earlier update point than the figures from Congolese health authorities reported by AP on October 31. The WHO tally sometimes treats suspected cases and cases under verification separately from confirmed cases, so national flash reports and international agency tallies may not match. Therefore, one should not conclude that "the WHO figures are wrong" or "the AP figures are wrong," but rather read them while considering the differences in update timing and aggregation methods. ● 9,704 confirmed cases as of the WHO's October 20 announcement In the situation report released by the WHO on October 23, there were 9,704 confirmed cases and 4,687 deaths in Congo as of October 20, with a crude case fatality rate of 48.3% among confirmed cases. This outbreak was declared on May 15, and since then, cases have been reported in 8 provinces and 66 health zones in Congo. While provinces are administrative units similar to prefectures in Japan, health zones are more granular regional divisions. The increase in numbers from the WHO report on October 20 to the AP report on October 31 is due to new cases and deaths added as the outbreak continues. ◆ What is the Bundibugyo Ebola strain? ● Ebola is not a term that refers to only one virus The infectious disease called "Ebola" is caused by multiple viruses included in the genus Orthoebolavirus. The main species listed by the WHO as causing disease in humans include Ebola virus, Sudan virus, Bundibugyo virus, and Taï Forest virus. What is spreading in Congo this time is an infectious disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus. The "Ebola virus," which caused major outbreaks in the past and for which vaccine development has progressed, and the current "Bundibugyo virus" are different species, even though they are in the same family. ● Even if the virus strain is different, immune response is not necessarily the same Vaccines aim to teach the immune system the characteristics of a specific pathogen to prevent infection and severe illness. If the marker parts on the surface of the virus are different, a vaccine made for one strain will not necessarily be sufficiently effective against another. For example, even if there is an approved vaccine for the Ebola virus, that does not mean its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus has been confirmed. What is being questioned in this outbreak is not just "whether an Ebola vaccine exists," but "whether the preventive effect against this specific virus has been verified in clinical trials and approved." ◆ Cases confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria ● Kenya confirms imported case and infection of contacts According to the WHO, an infection was confirmed in Kenya on October 6 in a person who had traveled from Congo. As of October 20, a total of 9 cases and 1 death were reported, and the additional 8 people were family members, medical workers, or caregivers whose epidemiological link to the initial case was confirmed. On the WHO dashboard dated October 28, confirmed deaths in Kenya were updated to 2. Since the death toll, which was 1 in the previous report, has changed, it is essential to include the aggregation date in articles and social media posts. As of October 20, the WHO stated there was no evidence of widespread community transmission outside of this cluster. ● One imported case in Nigeria In Nigeria, one infection was confirmed on October 20 in the Federal Capital Territory of Abuja. The patient had a travel history from Uganda, visited a medical institution after onset, and testing confirmed the Bundibugyo virus. In the WHO list as of October 28, Nigeria is also listed as having 1 confirmed case. Health authorities are identifying and monitoring contacts and continuing detailed investigations into the source of infection and travel/contact history. An imported case refers to a situation where a person infected outside the country where the infection is confirmed develops symptoms and is diagnosed after arriving, and it is not synonymous with the infection spreading among the residents of that country. ● Countries where related cases have been confirmed and countries where they have not According to WHO reports, cases related to this outbreak have been confirmed in five countries: Congo, Uganda, France, Kenya, and Nigeria. The situation varies by country; while a large-scale outbreak continues in Congo, a cluster resulting from an imported case was tracked in Kenya, and one imported case was reported in Nigeria. Cases in Uganda and France are treated as previously confirmed related cases on the WHO list, and the same scale of outbreak is not occurring in all countries. Japan is not included in the WHO list as of October 28, and based on the materials available in this article, it cannot be said that any cases related to this outbreak have been confirmed within Japan. ◆ Why Existing Ebola Vaccines Cannot Be Used ● Approved Vaccines Are for Specific Ebola Viruses According to the WHO, currently approved Ebola vaccines are intended for infections caused by the Ebola virus. There is no approved, specific vaccine for infections caused by the Bundibugyo virus. This does not mean it has been determined that 'all Ebola vaccines are useless.' Whether existing vaccines might offer cross-protection against this strain—that is, whether they might show some effectiveness against a different strain—needs to be confirmed through research. ● Clinical Trials for Candidate Vaccines Have Begun In May 2026, the WHO announced that a group of experts had recommended evaluating candidate vaccines and treatments through clinical trials. On October 23, the 'TUMAINI' Phase 2/3 trial began in Bunia, Congo, to investigate Bundibugyo strain vaccine candidates, and existing Ebola vaccines were also included as part of the candidate formulations for evaluation. Clinical trials are studies to verify safety, immune response, and actual preventive efficacy; the start of trials does not mean approval for general use. Regarding candidate drugs, the WHO also explains that they are at the stage of being evaluated within a research framework. ● Medical Care Is Provided Even While Vaccines Are Unavailable The fact that specific vaccines or treatments are not approved does not mean that medical care is not provided to patients. In the current response, supportive care, such as treatment based on symptoms, management of fluids and nutrition, and addressing complications, is important. The WHO explains that early diagnosis and connection to appropriate medical facilities are expected to improve patient conditions and reduce the risk of death. To suppress infection, it is necessary to combine testing, patient isolation and treatment, contact tracing, safe burials, and building trust with local residents. ◆ Conflict and Disruption of Medical Systems Make Containment Difficult ● Cases Where Patients Die Before Reaching Treatment Facilities The WHO identifies as a challenge the fact that many of the confirmed deaths occur within the community rather than in treatment facilities. As of October 20, out of 482 deaths confirmed in the most recent reporting period, 307 died in the community and 175 died in treatment facilities. When many people die at home or in the community before diagnosis, it becomes difficult to identify contacts and confirm transmission routes, and the opportunity for surrounding people to take infection control measures is delayed. Because fever and fatigue in the early stages of onset are also seen in other diseases such as malaria, it is difficult to determine it is Ebola based on symptoms alone. ● Conflict and Attacks on Healthcare Workers Hinder Response In eastern Congo, the center of the outbreak, clashes with armed groups and deteriorating security continue, which are factors limiting the areas where healthcare workers and support teams can travel. The AP reported that attacks on medical facilities and those responding, distrust among residents, and delays in paying wages to healthcare workers are making containment difficult. If treatment facilities temporarily cease operations or necessary supplies and personnel do not arrive, the system for finding patients early and connecting them to treatment weakens. The success or failure of infection control depends not only on medical technology but also on an environment where healthcare workers can operate safely and a relationship where local residents can consult with peace of mind. ◆ How Is Ebola Transmitted? ● The Main Route of Infection Is Contact with Body Fluids of Infected Persons The WHO explains that human-to-human transmission of the Bundibugyo strain of Ebola occurs primarily through direct contact with the blood, secretions, organs, or other body fluids of an infected person, or with objects or environments contaminated with them. Even after a patient has died, there is a risk of infection from direct contact with the body or body fluids, so safe burial procedures are important. In medical settings, the risk of infection increases if appropriate infection prevention measures are insufficient. Infected persons are considered not to be infectious until symptoms appear during the incubation period indicated by the WHO. ● Do Not Be Afraid Based Only on the Image of Airborne Transmission Ebola is not described as a disease that spreads widely through everyday air, like measles or COVID-19. What WHO information emphasizes is contact with the body fluids or contaminated items of infected persons, infection control in medical settings, and contact during burials. Explanations such as 'it is dangerous just to be in the same space because it is airborne' do not correctly represent the main transmission routes currently confirmed. This does not mean that the risk of infection should be taken lightly, but it is important for people involved in medical care and funerals in outbreak areas to take appropriate protective measures. ◆ How Should We Think About the Impact on Japan? ● The WHO Does Not Recommend Uniform Restrictions on Travel and Trade Based on current information, the WHO does not recommend any uniform restrictions on travel or trade to the affected countries. This does not mean the outbreak is mild; rather, it is calling on countries to strengthen cross-border surveillance, early case detection, testing systems, and the preparedness of medical institutions. In its October 23 report, the WHO explained that it is in the process of re-evaluating the situation regarding its August 14 assessment, which rated the risk within Congo as 'very high,' the risk to countries bordering Congo as 'high,' and the risk to other countries in the African region and the world as 'low.' Therefore, the global risk assessment is not uniform and must be considered according to the distance from the outbreak area and the infection status. ● What people living in Japan should know Japan's cases are not listed in the WHO's tally as of October 28. While it is important to be informed about the situation in the affected regions, there is no need to interpret current data as meaning that the infection is spreading within Japan. What individuals can do is check the latest information released by the WHO and Japanese public institutions, and avoid spreading unverified numbers or posts claiming an 'explosion of infections across all of Africa.' Linking the danger of infectious diseases to people based on their place of origin, nationality, or skin color leads to discrimination and prejudice that are not based on facts. Infection control measures should be based on the actual local outbreak situation and medical risks. ◆ Do not oversimplify by saying 'but there is an Ebola vaccine' The background to the death toll exceeding 5,000 in Congo includes the lack of approved vaccines or specific treatments for the Bundibugyo strain, the difficulty of early symptom detection, patients dying before reaching medical facilities, and the obstruction of medical workers' activities due to conflict and attacks. The extent to which existing Ebola vaccines are effective against this virus is still at the stage of being verified through trials, and they cannot be treated as having already been confirmed to have sufficient efficacy. The mere fact that there is no vaccine does not make treatment or infection control measures meaningless. The pillars of current containment efforts are the accumulation of testing, isolation, supportive care, contact tracing, and cooperation with local residents. It is important not to lump this outbreak together as a 'crisis for all of Africa,' but to carefully look at which country has confirmed what, and at what point in time the figures were recorded. Furthermore, when reading news about infectious diseases, we should also pay attention to the situations faced by patients, medical workers, and local residents. We are called upon to remember the people behind the numbers, avoid spreading prejudice, and follow the situation based on updated public information. Main sources WHO 'Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus, Democratic Republic of the Congo, Kenya and Nigeria' WHO 'Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak dashboard' WHO 'Ebola vaccines' WHO 'Experts convened by WHO advise on candidate treatments and vaccines for Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus' AP News 'Congo’s Ebola deaths top 5,000 as fear of continent-wide spread mounts' #CongoEbola #BundibugyoEbola #EbolaVaccine #WHO #SGNewsJapan #WHO #SGNewsJAPAN #エボラワクチン #コンゴエボラ #ブンディブギョ型エボラ #WHO #SGNewsJAPAN #エボラワクチン #コンゴエボラ #ブンディブギョ型エボラ トップ 健康・医療 病気 あなたへのおすすめ ピックアップされています いまネットで起きていること 30件

Time
The outbreak in Congo was declared on May 15, 2026, with updated figures on October 20, October 28, and October 31, 2026. The publication date of the news report is November 2, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is primarily in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, with cases confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria. The affected population includes individuals in 8 provinces and 66 health zones in Congo, as well as contacts in Kenya and Nigeria.
Event
An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus has been reported in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, with cases also confirmed in Kenya and Nigeria. The event is confirmed by health authorities and the WHO, with specific details about the strain and vaccine limitations.
Scale, impact and risk
As of October 31, 2026, there were 10,351 infections and 5,026 deaths in Congo. The WHO reported 10,274 confirmed cases and 4,992 deaths as of October 28, 2026. The crude case fatality rate among confirmed cases was 48.3% as of October 20, 2026. The outbreak has not resulted in widespread community transmission outside the initial cluster, according to WHO reports.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health authorities are identifying and monitoring contacts, and detailed investigations are ongoing. The WHO and Congolese health authorities are providing updated figures, but there are differences in aggregation methods and update timing. The data is based on confirmed cases and does not include suspected cases.

However, the supplied evidence does not include specific details about the number of cases, deaths, or the scale of the outbreak. The report emphasizes the challenges faced by individuals living with HIV and other vulnerable groups in the region, highlighting the socio-economic and stigmatization issues that impact healthcare access. The content is part of MSF's general advocacy and humanitarian activities, with no explicit mention of confirmed or suspected cases beyond the individual case of Victoire.

联合国儿童基金会在邻国加强应对埃博拉暴发的准备工作 - Unicef

UNICEF scales up preparedness in response to Ebola outbreaks in neighbouring countries - Unicef
新闻简报联合国儿童基金会(UNICEF)已加强在南苏丹的准备工作,以应对邻国,特别是刚果民主共和国(DRC)中埃博拉病毒(暴发)的威胁。截至11月1日,刚果民主共和国已有超过10,000人感染邦迪布古亚病毒病(BVD),导致超过5,000人死亡。儿童受到的影响尤为严重,其中四分之一的病例和三分之一的死亡均为儿童。该机构已运送超过50公吨的物资,包括个人防护装备和感染控制材料,并开展了一次全国性的模拟演练,以评估应对能力。联合国儿童基金会南苏丹代表诺拉·斯金纳(Noala Skinner)强调,需要增加投资以加强基于社区的监测、医疗机构准备度以及跨境应对能力。最近的暴发已经对南苏丹的基本服务造成压力,凸显了在国内投资社会服务以支持准备工作和应对行动的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

UNICEF scales up preparedness in response to Ebola outbreaks in neighbouring countries - Unicef

News Brief
UNICEF has intensified its preparedness efforts in South Sudan to address the threat of Ebola outbreaks in neighboring countries, particularly the Democratic Republic of Congo. As of November 1, over 10,000 people have been infected with Bundibugyo Virus Disease (BVD) in the DRC, resulting in more than 5,000 deaths. Children are disproportionately affected, with one in four cases and one in three deaths being among them. The agency has delivered over 50 metric tonnes of supplies, including personal protective equipment and infection control materials, and conducted a national simulation exercise to evaluate response capabilities. UNICEF South Sudan Representative, Noala Skinner, emphasized the need for additional investment to strengthen community-based surveillance, health facility readiness, and cross-border preparedness. The recent outbreak has already strained essential services in South Sudan, highlighting the importance of domestic investment in social services to support preparedness and response efforts.

UNICEF scales up preparedness in response to Ebola outbreaks in neighbouring countries Skip to main content South Sudan Toggle navigation Visit UNICEF Global South Sudan Explore UNICEF About us Our partners Our representative Contact us Press centre Donate South Sudan What we do Research and reports Stories Take action Search area has closed. Show results from: Clear search input Search for result Max Clear search input Search for result Press release UNICEF scales up preparedness in response to Ebola outbreaks in neighbouring countries 03 November 2026 UNICEF South Sudan A community mobilizer shares information on the signs and symptoms of Ebola with road users in Western Equatoria State, South Sudan, using an awareness poster to promote early recognition and encourage communities to take preventive measures JUBA, South Sudan, 3 November 2026 - UNICEF South Sudan is on alert for further outbreaks of Ebola, Bundibugyo Virus Disease (BVD) following cases in neighbouring countries, including the Democratic Republic of Congo, and is scaling up preparedness in priority counties. The scale up is generously supported by donors including the United States Government, Mastercard Foundation and the UN Central Emergency Revolving Fund (CERF). The UN children’s agency has delivered over 50 metric tonnes of supplies including Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) and materials for infection control (IPC). In some of the most ‘at risk’ counties and states, UNICEF is strengthening preparedness by improving infection prevention standards in health facilities and communities, and equipping health workers, rapid response teams, faith leaders, and community members with the knowledge and skills needed to identify symptoms, promote protective behaviours, support early referral, and combat misinformation related to BVD. A key preparedness milestone was the recent national simulation exercise to test South Sudan’s capacity to detect and manage any potential cases or outbreaks. Led by the National Public Health Institute, Ministry of Health, the simulation exercise identified strengths and gaps in a system wide response. Dr Douglas Noble, UNICEF Global Lead for Public Health Emergencies and Incident Manager for the Ebola Crisis, visited South Sudan shortly after the simulation and met with partners and Government officials A great deal of important BVD preparedness work has been undertaken by UNICEF, WHO, Africa CDC and partners in support of the Government. The recent simulation exercise was an important step forward. The priority now must be to address remaining gaps in the days and weeks ahead. UNICEF South Sudan Representative, Noala Skinner has underscored that additional investment is essential to strengthen community-based surveillance, improve health facility readiness, maintain supply pipelines, enhance cross-border preparedness, and increase surge capacity before any outbreak occurs. Evidence from previous Ebola outbreaks shows that preparation is key to keeping outbreaks under control. Domestic investment in the social services, including payment of salaries could not be more important at this time to underpin preparedness and support any response. Skinner says ongoing crises in the country, including displacement, flooding, drought-like conditions, food insecurity and recurrent disease outbreaks, are already straining essential services and increasing the risk of public health emergencies. Bundibugyo is a rare and different type of Ebola and currently has no specific treatment, though trials and tests are underway to find effective medicines and vaccines. As of November 1, more than 10,000 people were infected with BVD in DRC, where the response is struggling to contain the disease. Of those more than 5,000 have died. Children have been particularly affected by the outbreak in DRC, with one in four cases being children and one in three deaths. In the event of an outbreak in South Sudan, skilled and trained health workers, sufficient IPC supplies and well-informed communities are key to containing and treating patients and preventing cases from growing exponentially. Together with our partners, we work in 190 countries and territories to translate that commitment into practical action, focusing special effort on reaching the most vulnerable and excluded children, to the benefit of all children, everywhere. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国最致命的埃博拉病毒 暴发 病例已突破 10,000 例,但尚未获得批准的 Bundibugyo 治疗 或 疫苗 - 《拉丁时报》

DRC's deadliest Ebola outbreak has passed 10,000 cases, with no approved Bundibugyo treatment or vaccine - Latin Times
新闻简报The DRC is experiencing its deadliest and fastest-growing Ebola outbreak, with 10,060 confirmed cases and 4,887 deaths as of November 3, 2026. The outbreak is driven by the Bundibugyo virus, for which no approved vaccine or treatment exists, making it a significant public health challenge. The virus has spread across 66 health zones and eight provinces, with cases confirmed in Uganda, France, Kenya, and Nigeria. Uganda resolved all 20 infections by late October 2026, while Kenya and Nigeria reported cases in October 2026. The case fatality rate is near 49 percent, and the outbreak now trails only the 2014–2016 West Africa crisis in total mortality. Despite expanded treatment capacity, challenges remain due to patient distrust and limited healthcare infrastructure. A race for a countermeasure is underway, with 70,000 doses of Ervebo (approved for a different strain) dispatched and early human trials for Bundibugyo-specific vaccines launched by Oxford University and Moderna.
查看新闻五要素
时间
自2026年5月15日以来,暴发已在八个省份造成4,887人死亡,截至2026年11月3日确诊病例达到10,060例。肯尼亚和尼日利亚于2026年10月报告了病例,乌干达当时解决了所有20例感染。
地点与对象
The Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), specifically eight provinces and 66 health zones, with Ituri Province accounting for over 73 percent of cumulative cases. The affected population includes individuals in these regions, with no specific mention of animals or hosts.
事件
自 2026 年 5 月 15 日以来,刚果民主共和国已记录了 10,060 例埃博拉确诊病例,其中 4,887 人因本迪布焦病毒死亡。这是该国有记录以来最致命、增长最快的 暴发,目前尚未批准用于 Bundibugyo 菌株的 疫苗 或 治疗。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has spread across 66 health zones and eight provinces in the DRC, with confirmed imported cases in Uganda, France, Kenya, and Nigeria. The case fatality rate is near 49 percent, and the outbreak has already surpassed the 10,000-case threshold, making it the third documented Bundibugyo outbreak in history and the largest by a significant margin.
应对、状态与不确定性
A race for a countermeasure is underway, with 70,000 doses of Ervebo (approved for a different strain) dispatched to the DRC in August. Oxford University and Moderna have launched early human trials for Bundibugyo-specific vaccines. Contact tracing efforts have improved, but the Africa CDC has set 95 percent traceability as the benchmark for control, which the outbreak has not yet met. Response teams have expanded treatment capacity, but challenges persist due to patient distrust and limited healthcare infrastructure.

The case fatality rate is near 49 percent, and the outbreak now trails only the 2014–2016 West Africa crisis in total mortality. Despite expanded treatment capacity, challenges remain due to patient distrust and limited healthcare infrastructure. A race for a countermeasure is underway, with 70,000 doses of Ervebo (approved for a different strain) dispatched and early human trials for Bundibugyo-specific vaccines launched by Oxford University and Moderna.
Original source text (English)
Tuesday, November 3, 2026 S&P ▲0.30%Dow ▼0.44%Nasdaq ▲0.62% English Search form Search Politics Immigration Business Entertainment Sports Culture Lifestyle Video World DRC Ebola: 10,000 Cases, Nearly 5,000 Dead — Why Kenya and Nigeria Are Now a Cause for Alarm The outbreak has killed 4,887 people across eight provinces, while imported cases and early-stage vaccine trials intensify pressure on health authorities. Mateo Moreno / Published Oct 29 2026, 8:19 AM EDT Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on WhatsApp Volunteers of the Democratic Republic of Congo Red Cross wearing personal protective equipment load the casket containing the body of an Ebola virus disease victim at the morgue of the Rwampara health centre, into a vehicle for transfer to a cemetery as part of safe and dignified burial operations aimed at preventing the spread of the virus. June 8, 2026. Photo by Jospin Mwisha / AFP via Getty Images The Briefing The DRC has recorded 10,060 confirmed Ebola cases and 4,887 deaths since May 15 — its deadliest and fastest-growing outbreak on record — driven by the Bundibugyo virus, for which no approved vaccine or treatment exists. The outbreak has spread across 66 health zones and eight provinces in the DRC, with confirmed imported cases in Uganda, France, Kenya, and Nigeria; Uganda has since resolved all 20 of its infections, while Kenya and Nigeria only reported cases in October 2026. A race for a countermeasure is underway: 70,000 doses of Ervebo — approved against a different Ebola strain — were dispatched to the DRC in August, while Oxford University and Moderna have launched separate early human trials of Bundibugyo-specific vaccines. The Democratic Republic of Congo has crossed a threshold that public health officials had feared since May: government data released Tuesday confirmed 10,060 Ebola infections and 4,887 deaths since the outbreak was formally declared on May 15 — making this the deadliest and fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in the country's recorded history. On a global scale, it now trails only the 2014–2016 West Africa crisis in total Ebola mortality, a catastrophe that killed more than 11,000 people across 28,600 confirmed cases. With a case fatality rate hovering near 49 percent, few modern disease events carry a comparable death toll among those infected. A Pathogen the World Has No Approved Answer For What sets this outbreak apart from all 16 previous DRC Ebola crises is the pathogen itself. While the Zaire ebolavirus was eventually brought under control across every prior DRC episode, this surge is driven by the Bundibugyo virus, a rarer species with only two prior outbreaks on record globally: one in Uganda in 2007–2008 and one in the DRC in 2012. The current episode is therefore the third documented Bundibugyo outbreak in history — and already its largest by an enormous margin. The operational weight of that distinction is severe. No approved vaccine or treatment exists for the Bundibugyo strain, stripping frontline responders of the pharmacological tools that helped contain previous outbreaks. The disease has spread from three affected health zones in May to 66 zones spanning eight provinces, with Ituri Province — the declared epicenter — accounting for more than 73 percent of cumulative cases, followed by North Kivu. Both regions are already battered by armed conflict, generational poverty, and healthcare infrastructure operating far below minimum capacity. Volunteers of the Democratic Republic of Congo Red Cross wearing personal protective equipment disinfect themselves in a doffing area after removing the body of an Ebola virus disease victim from the morgue of the Rwampara health centre, Ituri Province, Democratic Republic of Congo, on June 8, 2026 before loading it into a vehicle for transfer to a cemetery as part of safe and dignified burial operations aimed at preventing the spread of the virus. Photo by Jospin Mwisha / AFP via Getty Images Transmission Far Outrunning the Response One figure from July 2026 data exposes why containment has proven so elusive: more than 80 percent of new confirmed cases were appearing outside the contact chains health teams were actively monitoring. The Africa CDC has set 95 percent contact traceability as the benchmark for an outbreak to be considered substantially under control — a threshold the epidemic remained far from meeting for months, even as tracing rates improved gradually through the second half of 2026. Even identifying a patient is only half the challenge. A CDC analysis published in late August found that more than half of confirmed deaths were occurring outside Ebola treatment units — a figure that WHO reporting, cited by NPR in July, had placed closer to two-thirds. The divergence reflects the trajectory: the situation was more dire in the early months. Response teams have substantially expanded treatment capacity since then, growing from roughly 700 beds across 22 facilities in early July to a significantly larger network now deployed across the affected provinces. Yet no amount of infrastructure compensates when patients cannot be reached or refuse to seek care, driven by deep-seated distrust toward health authorities rooted in years of conflict and failed institutional promises. A Crisis That Has Spread Across Borders The outbreak is no longer contained within the DRC's borders. Since May 15, five countries have confirmed Bundibugyo virus cases: the DRC, Uganda, France, Kenya, and Nigeria. Uganda recorded 20 imported infections and two deaths through the summer, but health officials confirmed no active cases remain in the country. France reported a single imported case on June 24 in a humanitarian physician returning from Ituri Province; no secondary transmission occurred in Europe. Kenya recorded its first confirmed case in early October, and Nigeria followed on October 20, both linked to travel from the DRC. The World Health Organization has warned that Africa faces an unprecedented containment challenge, with Bundibugyo virus now simultaneously present across the continent's eastern, central, and western zones. Violence Against Responders Compounds the Crisis Epidemiological pressure is compounded by deliberate violence against the people trying to stop the outbreak. In late October, a nurse was killed and a health worker severely injured responding to an Ebola alert in Tshopo Province — one incident in a pattern that has stretched across multiple provinces since May. The International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies has documented attacks on volunteers and staff over dozens of incidents, including destroyed equipment, burned vehicles, and volunteers killed, as armed groups and community resistance continue to obstruct response operations. Borrowed Tools and the Race Toward a Countermeasure With no Bundibugyo-specific treatment available, global health authorities have turned to what exists. On July 31, WHO's Technical Advisory Group on Candidate Vaccine Prioritization convened its third meeting and recommended that Ervebo — the only licensed Ebola vaccine in the world, proven against the Zaire strain — be evaluated in a Phase 3 clinical trial to determine whether it might offer cross-protection against Bundibugyo. The recommendation drew on preliminary animal data suggesting partial protective potential. WHO has been explicit that human efficacy against Bundibugyo virus remains unproven. On August 20, the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision released 70,000 Ervebo doses to the DRC from the global stockpile — 20,000 allocated for the Phase 3 clinical trial and 50,000 prioritized for frontline healthcare workers. In parallel, the University of Oxford's Vaccine Group launched the world's first Phase 1 human trial of a Bundibugyo-specific vaccine in July, using the same viral vector technology as the Oxford-AstraZeneca COVID-19 jab. Moderna began its own Phase 1 mRNA-based Bundibugyo trial in Canada in early August. Both remain at the safety and immune-response stage, meaning a proven, strain-specific countermeasure is still months or years away. Children, Maternal Care, and a System Beyond Its Limits Behind the aggregate numbers lie quieter catastrophes. Since late May, at least one in four confirmed Ebola deaths has been a child, according to Save the Children — and UNICEF reported in August that children now account for almost one in three fatalities, with children under five dying at more than double the rate seen among adults. Routine childhood vaccination campaigns have been suspended or severely disrupted across affected zones, and maternal mortality doubled in some provinces within weeks of the outbreak declaration, as a healthcare system consumed by a single overwhelming emergency ceased to provide essential services to everyone else. In five months, this outbreak has expanded at more than three times the pace of the 2014–2016 West Africa epidemic — still history's deadliest, with 28,600 cases and over 11,000 deaths. Whether the DRC eventually eclipses those totals depends entirely on what the coming weeks produce. What the data already establishes is that 10,000 cases is not a ceiling. For a virus still spreading across eight provinces, active in four neighboring countries, and still largely invisible to surveillance teams in the areas hardest to reach, it may be closer to a turning point — in whatever direction the response can now force. 新闻简报刚果民主共和国(DRC)出现了一种新的暴发,该病毒已导致超过10,000例确诊病例和近5,000例死亡,死亡率高达50%。这种暴发主要集中在DRC的Ituri省,并已传播到邻国如乌干达、肯尼亚和尼日利亚,以及中国和美国等其他地区。该病毒被鉴定为邦迪布古病毒(Bundibugyo virus),是一种新出现的毒株,令科学界措手不及,因为它缺乏特定的疫苗或治疗。DRC已启动应急响应措施,并正在开展针对潜在疫苗的临床试验,但专家估计,新的疫苗可能要到明年才能面世。目前,一线医务人员正在依靠基础的支持性治疗,如补水和血压管理,来治疗患者。该暴发的快速传播以及缺乏有效的防控措施,引发了对其对公共卫生和医疗体系影响的广泛关注。世界卫生组织(WHO)已将此次暴发暴发列为国际公共卫生紧急事件(PHEIC),突显了局势的严重性以及需要全球关注的必要性。

Original title (Chinese)
非洲埃博拉病毒感染确诊破1万,死亡率高达50%!已蔓延多国...-yeeyi - yeeyi
News Brief
A new outbreak of Ebola virus disease has emerged in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), with over 10,000 confirmed cases and nearly 5,000 deaths, resulting in a death rate of 50%. This outbreak, centered in the Ituri province of DRC, has spread to neighboring countries such as Uganda, Kenya, and Nigeria, as well as to other regions like China and the United States. The virus, identified as the Bundibugyo virus, is a new strain that has caught the scientific community off guard, as it lacks a specific vaccine or treatment. The DRC has activated emergency response measures and is conducting clinical trials for a potential vaccine, but experts estimate that a new vaccine may not be available until next year. Currently, frontline medical personnel are relying on basic supportive care, such as hydration and blood pressure management, to treat patients. The outbreak's rapid spread and the lack of effective containment measures have raised significant concerns about its impact on public health and the healthcare system. The World Health Organization (WHO) has classified this outbreak as an international public health emergency (PHEIC), highlighting the severity of the situation and the need for global attention.

科学界在过去十年里对扎伊尔型毒株(Zaire)进行了深度研究,无论是2014年,还是2018年那次前第二大暴发期全都是扎伊尔型毒株。 扎伊尔型毒株致死率非常吓人,常高达六到九成。 由于跟扎伊尔型打交道很深,该毒株杀伤力太强,所以医学界前沿科学聚集全员之力研发了针对该毒株的首款埃博拉疫苗(Ervebo)。 据统计,今年以前,全球95%以上的埃博拉科研经费、疫苗研发和药物储备都死锁在扎伊尔型上面,对这次疫情的毒株罕见的本迪布焦型埃博拉病毒(Bundibugyo virus)完全没有特效药或者疫苗。 之前科学界认为其他埃博拉病毒株型不会造成大面积严重后果疫情,然而这次本迪布焦型毒株打了人类一个措手不及。 本迪布焦型是2007年11月的一轮埃博拉疫情中被人类首次发现的,当时乌干达卫生部和世卫组织工作人员在对当地患者进行采血基因测序时,发现这是一种此前从未被记录过的全新埃博拉病毒株(目前总共有大约四五种埃博拉病毒株)。 刚果(金)近期在国内启动了代号为“希望”的候选疫苗临床试验,评估现有扎伊尔型毒株疫苗对本迪布焦型毒株的保护力并进行调整改造。 但远水解不了近渴,由于临床研发需要时间和经验,可能要到明年才能有真正能投入广泛使用的新疫苗出现。 目前医疗人员在前线大多只能依靠基础的补液、维持血压来帮患者硬扛。 由于无法通过以往那样大量接种形成短期内的人群免疫屏障,专家估计这波来势汹汹的本迪布焦型疫情可能要持续数月,延续到2027年。 目前刚果(金)的防疫形势严峻,由于一万例确诊里面仅有12%的病患存在社区联系,大量病患都呈点状分布。 很多还没发病的病毒携带者正在进行随机迁移务工,对自己体内的可怕病毒一无所知,这对疫情的监控非常不利。 目前刚果(金)附近国家、我国和美国等国已经严格监管起入境人员的旅行史和体温,尽力防止病毒被带入国境线之内。 而在刚果(金),由于震中地区的武装冲突和社区对防疫人员的抵触(偏远地区存在对官方的不信任和对医护人员不归还遗体的恐惧),如今防疫难度大大增加。 目前,世界卫生组织(WHO)已将本轮疫情列为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。 感染基数太大,病毒携带者又在全国甚至非洲各地自由随机流动,缺乏特效药疫苗,疫情中心区抗疫设施和条件有限,部分地区民众不配合,极其快速的传播速度都让这次疫情的前景令人担忧。 1976年,埃博拉病毒首次在刚果(金)的埃博拉河附近被发现,如今过了整整50年,刚果(金)的疫情又让人回到了当时人类无知盲目的状态。 无论如何,向奋斗在抗疫一线的人民致敬,也希望这种可怕的病毒能尽早被隔离在人类社会之外吧... *以上内容系网友创梦AUS自行转载自留学生大叔,该文仅代表原作者观点和态度。yeeyi号系信息发布平台,仅提供信息存储空间服务,不代表赞同其观点和对其真实性负责。如果对文章或图片/视频版权有异议,请邮件至support@yeeyi.com反馈,平台将会及时处理。 责任编辑:suri 分享到: 2收藏3点赞 相关推荐 澳洲经济,要完蛋了网购会更贵!机构呼吁澳洲向Temu、Sh...无语!Coles肉品货架摄像头居然是假的...刚刚,Aldi 官宣:取消这项做法,很多...真要关门?!珀斯这个David Jone...澳央行年内第5次加息; 阿德房价连跌5个... 评论0条 登录后评论 创梦AUS 普通用户 + 关注 yeeyi热搜 “活不下去了!!” 大批华人哀鸿遍野! 澳洲加息再创近18年新高! 有人忍痛卖房套现! 有人月供$6800彻夜难眠! 降息恐要等到明年底...热 重磅突发!澳洲官宣再加息,现金利率飙升至4.85%热 澳洲有两种华人大爷大妈,一种领着高昂的退休金生活富裕,一种在快餐店打黑工谋生。热 刚刚,维州政府重磅承诺:明年开20家免费牙科诊所!选址曝光! 突发!澳洲利率升到18年最高!RBA官宣第5次加息冲上4.85%!

提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告响应、状态或剩余的不确定性。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
非洲埃博拉病毒感染确诊破1万,死亡率高达50%!已蔓延多国...
News Brief
The Ebola virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo has confirmed over 10,000 cases with a mortality rate of 50%. The virus has spread to multiple countries including Uganda, Kenya, and Nigeria, raising concerns about further transmission. Despite emergency responses and international aid, there are no effective vaccines or treatments available, and the epidemic is expected to persist for several months.
Original source text (Chinese)
新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 非洲埃博拉病毒感染确诊破1万,死亡率高达50%!已蔓延多国... 2026-11-01 23:13 来源: 新中式的力量 防走丢,大家也可以在“ 事儿君看天下”找到我 关注起来,以后不“失联”~ 今年以来,非洲中部爆发了新一轮的埃博拉疫情。 不同以往的是,这次疫情爆发规模相当之大,目前已经超过2018年的数千例感染,成为了有史以来第二大埃博拉疫情。 更可怕的是,科学界目前对本轮疫情的毒株并不了解,根本没有相关疫苗和特效药,简直像在应对一种完全陌生的病毒... 非洲中部刚果(金)(即刚果民主共和国)东部的伊图里省是本轮疫情的核心疫区,该区域地处国界线上,靠近当地的原始森林,也是2018年第十轮埃博拉(当时的规模第二大)的核心震中。 截至目前,本次疫情累计确诊病例已经突破一万例,死亡人数高达近5千例,粗略死亡率高达50%。 展开全文 不仅感染基数大,这次疫情的蔓延速度令人乍舌。 在刚果(金)激活应急响应调动全国医疗资源外加世卫组织援助力量后,仅仅40天病例就飞涨了1000例,是前第二大疫情蔓延速度的3倍以上! 而随着国内外贸易商业等人员流动,刚果(金)周边的一些非洲国家接连遭殃。 乌干达、肯尼亚(确诊9例)和西非的尼日利亚(确诊1例)均发现了输入性病例。后两者的首例病例均有乌干达的旅行史,但毒株则和刚果(金)的疫情震中毒株测序相同,看起来乌干达是作为了第一个被传染的国家,之后病毒又从这里传到了别国。 媒体报道刚果(金)有跨国救援一线人员,甚至外交官家属被感染,鉴于疫区医疗条件紧张且比较基础,他们已经经由医疗直升机被转移到欧洲的大医院接受看护。 从过往的例子来看,这些被转移的病例由于能接受欧洲大医院的顶级医学设备和精细化护理,致死率可能“只有”10-20%左右。 而无法转移的本地患病民众的致死率则高得多,从过往的记录来说约有1/4至1/2的死亡率。 埃博拉病毒一直以极度凶残的致死率闻名,一旦病毒载量过高,病人几乎无法生还。 而这也造成埃博拉相对小范围的传播——患者一旦发病且具有传染性时一般都是病入膏肓了,而且埃博拉必须通过体液血液进行传播,无法在空气中直接传播,所以很多病毒快速杀死了人体宿主,继而自己也因为失去宿主而死亡。 世上第一次也是最严重的埃博拉超级大爆发从2014年开始,持续了两年,聚集爆发在西非多个国家,从小村最后蔓延到人口密集的国际化大都市,最终导致超2.8万人感染,超1.1万人死亡。 12年过去了,为什么如今这波埃博拉又突然变得如此严重,病例快速积累到1万,死亡人数也破5千了呢? 科学界在过去十年里对扎伊尔型毒株(Zaire)进行了深度研究,无论是2014年,还是2018年那次前第二大暴发期全都是扎伊尔型毒株。 扎伊尔型毒株致死率非常吓人,常高达六到九成。 由于跟扎伊尔型打交道很深,该毒株杀伤力太强,所以医学界前沿科学聚集全员之力研发了针对该毒株的首款埃博拉疫苗(Ervebo)。 据统计,今年以前,全球95%以上的埃博拉科研经费、疫苗研发和药物储备都死锁在扎伊尔型上面,对这次疫情的毒株罕见的 本迪布焦型 埃博拉病毒(Bundibugyo virus)完全没有特效药或者疫苗。 之前科学界认为其他埃博拉病毒株型不会造成大面积严重后果疫情,然而这次本迪布焦型毒株打了人类一个措手不及。 本迪布焦型是2007年11月的一轮埃博拉疫情中被人类首次发现的,当时乌干达卫生部和世卫组织工作人员在对当地患者进行采血基因测序时,发现这是一种此前从未被记录过的全新埃博拉病毒株(目前总共有大约四五种埃博拉病毒株)。 刚果(金)近期在国内启动了代号为“希望”的候选疫苗临床试验,评估现有扎伊尔型毒株疫苗对 本迪布焦型毒株的保护力并进行调整改造。 但远水解不了近渴,由于临床研发需要时间和经验,可能要到明年才能有真正能投入广泛使用的新疫苗出现。 目前医疗人员在前线大多只能依靠基础的补液、维持血压来帮患者硬扛。 由于无法通过以往那样大量接种形成短期内的人群免疫屏障,专家估计这波来势汹汹的 本迪布焦型疫情可能要持续数月,延续到2027年。 目前刚果(金)的防疫形势严峻,由于一万例确诊里面仅有12%的病患存在社区联系,大量病患都呈点状分布。 很多还没发病的病毒携带者正在进行随机迁移务工,对自己体内的可怕病毒一无所知,这对疫情的监控非常不利。 目前刚果(金)附近国家、我国和美国等国已经严格监管起入境人员的旅行史和体温,尽力防止病毒被带入国境线之内。 而在刚果(金),由于震中地区的武装冲突和社区对防疫人员的抵触(偏远地区存在对官方的不信任和对医护人员不归还遗体的恐惧),如今防疫难度大大增加。 目前,世界卫生组织(WHO)已将本轮疫情列为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。 感染基数太大,病毒携带者又在全国甚至非洲各地自由随机流动,缺乏特效药疫苗,疫情中心区抗疫设施和条件有限,部分地区民众不配合,极其快速的传播速度都让这次疫情的前景令人担忧。 1976年,埃博拉病毒首次在刚果(金)的埃博拉河附近被发现,如今过了整整50年,刚果(金)的疫情又让人回到了当时人类无知盲目的状态。 无论如何,向奋斗在抗疫一线的人民致敬,也希望这种可怕的病毒能尽早被隔离在人类社会之外吧... Ref: https://nypost.com/2026/10/21/world-news/nigeria-reports-ebola-case-fourth-african-country-affected-by-outbreak/ 作者提示:素材来源官方媒体/网络新闻,文中事件发生于26年10月30日,刚果(金)返回搜狐,查看更多 阅读 () 平台声明 该文观点仅代表作者本人,搜狐号系信息发布平台,搜狐仅提供信息存储空间服务。

刚果埃博拉死亡人数已达 5,000 人,对整个非洲大陆蔓延的担忧加剧 - WRAL

Congo's Ebola deaths top 5,000 as worry of continent-wide spread mounts - WRAL
新闻简报刚果正在经历严重的埃博拉病毒 暴发,截至 2026 年 10 月 31 日,据报告已有 5,000 多人死亡。暴发 由本迪布焦病毒引起,于 5 月中旬宣布,控制一直面临挑战。根据政府最新数据,10,351例感染者中,已有5,026人死亡,致死率接近50%。此次暴发是历史上第二致命的暴发,仅次于2014-2016年西非暴发。该病毒向其他非洲国家的传播引起了人们对可能出现在整个非洲大陆的 传播 的担忧。专家们担心当局控制该病毒的能力,该病毒已经造成了重大的公共卫生挑战。该情况仍在积极调查中,目前还没有明确的解决方案。
查看新闻五要素
时间
周六最新的政府数据显示,刚果埃博拉病毒 暴发 的死亡人数已突破 5,000 人,该国正在努力应对该疾病的不断蔓延,并且其他非洲国家也出现了病例。最新的 暴发 由 Bundibugyo 病毒引起,于 5 月中旬宣布,令当局感到惊讶。从那以后,他们一直在努力管理它。病例激增引发了专家们对当局遏制这种致命病毒能力的担忧。
地点与对象
刚果,特别是布尼亚,受影响的人群是普通民众和医护人员。暴发 已传播到其他非洲国家。
事件
刚果埃博拉病毒 暴发 已导致 5,000 多人死亡,该疾病由本迪布焦病毒引起。暴发于5月中旬宣布,一直难以管理。专家们对病毒在整个非洲大陆的传播感到担忧。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 已导致 10,351 名感染者中有 5,026 人死亡,致死率接近 50%。这是历史上第二致命的埃博拉病毒暴发,仅次于2014年至2016年期间西非的暴发。该疾病已传播到其他非洲国家,引发了对整个非洲大陆传播的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
刚果卫生部提供了有关 暴发 的数据,政府正在努力应对该疾病的传播。专家们对当局遏制病毒的能力表示担忧。暴发 正在进行中,并且很难管理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Congo's Ebola deaths top 5,000 as worry of continent-wide spread mounts - WRAL

News Brief
Congo is experiencing a severe Ebola outbreak, with over 5,000 deaths reported as of October 31, 2026. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, was declared in mid-May and has been challenging to control. According to the latest government data, 5,026 people have died out of 10,351 infections, resulting in a nearly 50% lethality rate. This outbreak is the second deadliest in history, following the 2014-2016 West Africa outbreak. The spread of the virus to other African countries has raised concerns about potential continent-wide transmission. Experts are worried about the authorities’ ability to contain the virus, which has already caused significant public health challenges. The situation remains under active investigation, with no clear resolution in sight.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国埃博拉 治疗 中心在致命的 暴发 中被烧毁 - Yeni Şafak

Ebola treatment center torched in DR Congo amid deadly outbreak - Yeni Şafak English
新闻简报An Ebola treatment center in Bafwasende, a rural town in the Democratic Republic of Congo's north-central Tsopo province, was torched by unidentified attackers on Saturday, 01/11/2026. The attack destroyed two rooms reserved for suspected Ebola cases, forcing medical staff to flee to Kisangani. Suspected cases were transferred to Nia-Nia in the neighboring Ituri province. This incident occurred just one week after a health worker was lynched in the same area, highlighting the growing hostility toward containment efforts. The Bundibugyo virus outbreak has resulted in over 5,000 deaths and more than 10,000 confirmed cases since mid-May, with health officials noting that community resistance remains a major obstacle. Local security guards intervened to stop the fire, but the situation underscores the ongoing challenges in managing the outbreak and protecting medical personnel.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生在周六,01/11/2026,周日。攻击报告是在周六的凌晨进行的,更新则是在同一天完成的。
地点与对象
袭击发生在刚果民主共和国中北部措波省的一个乡村小镇巴夫瓦森德。受影响人群包括该地区的医务人员和疑似埃博拉病例。
事件
刚果民主共和国中北部措波省的一个乡村小镇巴夫瓦森德的一个埃博拉 治疗 中心被身份不明的袭击者纵火焚烧。Yeni Şafak English 报道了这一事件,并确认该设施遭到袭击。
规模、影响与风险
这次袭击摧毁了两个专门用于疑似埃博拉病例的房间,迫使医务人员逃往基桑加尼。疑似病例已转移至邻近伊图里省的尼亚-尼亚。自 5 月中旬以来,由本迪布焦病毒引起的 暴发 已导致 5,000 多人死亡,确诊病例超过 10,000 例。卫生官员承认,社区抵制仍然是病毒遏制工作的重大障碍。
应对、状态与不确定性
当地保安设法介入并扑灭了火焰。医务人员撤离了该设施,疑似病例也被转移。这次袭击凸显了前线医务人员面临的致命敌意,卫生官员也注意到遏制工作中持续存在的挑战。来源是完整的原始报告,因此没有任何限制。

File photo Unidentified attackers burned an Ebola treatment facility in the Democratic Republic of Congo's north-central Tsopo province on Saturday, destroying two rooms reserved for suspected cases and forcing medical staff to flee to Kisangani amid mounting community resistance to containment efforts. Treatment center attacked Unidentified assailants torched an Ebola treatment center in Bafwasende, a rural town in the Democratic Republic of Congo's north-central Tsopo province, in the early hours of Saturday. Two rooms designated for suspected Ebola cases were set ablaze before local security guards managed to intervene and extinguish the flames, according to Marcel Likilo, the medical doctor overseeing the health zone. Medical personnel evacuated Likilo told reporters in the provincial capital Kisangani that doctors deployed to support the Ebola response had fled to the city following the attack. Suspected cases previously housed at the facility were transferred to Nia-Nia in the neighboring Ituri province, he added. Pattern of violence The arson occurred just one week after a mob lynched a health worker who was responding to an Ebola alert in the same area, underscoring the lethal hostility facing frontline medical personnel. Health officials have acknowledged that community resistance remains a significant obstacle to virus containment efforts in the region. Death toll exceeds 5,000 According to the latest Health Ministry figures, confirmed Ebola cases have exceeded 10,000 since mid-May, with more than 5,000 deaths recorded across affected areas. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus species, has persisted for months without signs of abating as authorities struggle to maintain operations amid ongoing security challenges. News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
刚果埃博拉疫情失控的原因(视频)
News Brief
The supplied evidence does not clearly report the event time in verified English. The supplied source does not clearly identify the location or affected population. The supplied evidence does not clearly report the central event in verified English. Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. The supplied source does not clearly report the response status or unresolved information.
Original source text (Chinese)
刚果埃博拉疫情失控的原因(视频) 瑞士资讯swissinfo.ch 2026-10-29 16:44《瑞士资讯》官方账号 问AI · 全球电子设备需求如何牵动矿区防疫难题? 保障员工的安全是重中之重。 SWI swissinfo 在刚果民主共和国东部的热带雨林和矿区,至今史上最严重的埃博拉疫情正在肆虐。一位在瑞士非政府组织Medair工作的医生讲述了这场疫情迄今未能得到有效控制的原因。 刚果民主共和国官方确认本轮埃博拉疫情暴发至今,已经过去五个月了。这是该国历史上的第17次埃博拉疫情,也是迄今最严重的一次疫情。“这场危机波及区域的规模非常大,”瑞士援助组织Medair的医生兼营养学家皮埃尔-奥利维耶·恩加乔莱(Pierre-Olivier Ngadjole)说:“疫情蔓延的范围比整个瑞士还要大。” 有关埃博拉病毒 埃博拉病毒病(Ebola Virus Disease, EVD)是一种严重的病毒性传染病,主要通过接触感染者或受感染动物的血液、体液等传播。患者通常会出现发热、乏力、呕吐、腹泻等症状,重症病例可能出现多器官衰竭甚至死亡。根据病毒毒株和医疗条件不同,病死率可达30%-90%。 自1976年首次被发现以来,埃博拉疫情几乎全部发生在非洲,尤其是刚果民主共和国、乌干达、几内亚、利比里亚和塞拉利昂等国。2014年-2016年西非埃博拉疫情曾造成逾2.8万人感染、超过1.1万人死亡,是历史上规模最大的疫情。 中国迄今尚未出现本土埃博拉疫情,也未发现持续的人际传播。不过,中国曾加强针对非洲入境人员的检疫和监测,以防病毒输入。 欧洲则偶有输入病例,例如2026年法国曾报告一例从刚果民主共和国返回后确诊的埃博拉病例,但并未引发社区传播。由于医疗体系较完善,欧洲发生大规模疫情的风险被认为极低。 这次刚果民主共和国暴发的是本迪布焦型(Bundibugyo)埃博拉病毒疫情。与较常见的扎伊尔型(Zaire)埃博拉病毒相比,目前尚无获批的针对性疫苗和特效疗法,这也增加了疫情防控难度。 来源:世界卫生组织 据刚果国家公共卫生研究所(National Institute for Public Health)通报,目前已有超过9000例确诊感染病例,死亡人数超过4300人。在东部的伊图里(Ituri)省-疫情暴发地,以及北基伍(Nord-Kivu)和上韦莱(Haut-Uélé)两省,病毒仍在民众中蔓延。 最近的医院要跨越七条河 在布滕博(Butembo)卡特瓦(Katwa)埃博拉治疗中心,数字同样令人忧心。“在收治的252名患者中,已有70多人死亡,”恩加乔莱说。病死率约为48%。 在这位医生看来,高死亡率的主要原因并非病毒本身特别凶猛,而是患者接受治疗的时间过晚。许多感染者抵达医院时,往往已经错过最佳救治时机。 与此同时,患者群体也折射出当地的社会结构特征。受疫情影响最严重的是15岁-49岁人群,这与矿区营地密集的劳动活动密切相关。幼童则往往在家庭内部的日常接触中迅速受到感染。 前往疫情暴发地区本身就是一场艰难跋涉。“当地道路状况极其恶劣,一到雨季,连摩托车都难以通行,”恩加乔莱说。有些患者需要辗转数日、跨越多条河流才能抵达医疗机构。由于交通条件匮乏,不少家庭只能先依靠土方或传统疗法应对病情,结果往往错过最佳救治时机。 在民众中普及卫生教育是抗击埃博拉疫情的重要组成部分。 SWI swissinfo 谣言如何加剧疫情蔓延 除了地理上的隔绝之外,疫情初期还出现了一次代价惨重的信息传播失误。当时广泛流传着一种说法,认为针对当前这一病毒毒株既没有疫苗,也没有治愈方法。许多人都想“既然如此,为什么还要去医院?”恩加乔莱说:“这种误导的信息严重削弱了民众的抗疫意愿。” 事实上,目前针对此次疫情所涉及的本迪布焦型(Bundibugyo)病毒变种,的确尚无获批准的特效疗法。但及早接受重症医疗护理,例如通过输液补充电解质,能够显著提高患者的生存几率。同时,在医院进行隔离治疗,也有助于切断病毒传播链。 但雪上加霜的是,当地安全局势极其恶劣。许多感染者担心会在前往医院途中遭到武装民兵袭击,因而不敢冒险。此外,一些宗教团体鼓励患者通过祈祷应对疾病,而不是寻求医务治疗。因此,越来越多的人道主义组织,包括Medair,号召康复者参与到宣传和沟通工作中来,以建立公众信任。恩加乔莱说:“我们会让幸存者现身说法。” Medair是一家总部位于瑞士沃州的国际紧急救援组织,致力于在危机地区提供医疗和基础设施援助。该组织于1989年由两位医生及多名志愿者创立,如今运营收入约为9160万美元,在全球拥有1500多名员工。在瑞士,其资金来源包括瑞士发展与合作署(DEZA);根据2025年年度报告,欧盟人道主义援助办公室(ECHO)和美国国际开发署(USAID)也为其提供资助。刚果籍医生皮埃尔-奥利维耶·恩加乔莱(Pierre-Olivier Ngadjole)目前担任Medair在当地的顾问。 追踪密接者困难重重 不过,巴黎知名传染病学专家泽维尔·莱斯屈尔(Xavier Lescure)认为,疫情迟迟无法得到控制,还有另一个关键原因,那就是接触者追踪工作不到位。“我们必须完整追溯每一条传播链,”这位法国流行病风险协调任务负责人、世界卫生组织(WHO)国际研究网络联合协调人表示。目前,刚果对密切接触者的追踪覆盖率约为75%。 “在这样的条件下,这已经是相当了不起的成绩了,但仍远远不够。如果想真正止住疫情,覆盖率必须达到95%以上,”莱斯屈尔在接受瑞士资讯swissinfo.ch采访时说。他还确信,当地的信息环境也是导致疫情恶化的重要因素。他将其称为一种“后真相时代的疫情”:毫无依据的传言和普遍的不信任感正在削弱公共卫生措施的效果。 所以,现在只能把希望寄托于医学上的进展。莱斯屈尔表示,目前主要有两条途径:一方面,研究人员正在测试专门针对本迪布焦型(Bundibugyo)病毒开发的候选疫苗;另一方面,现有针对扎伊尔型(Zaire)病毒的rVSV疫苗也已投入使用。“初步科学证据显示,两种病毒株之间可能存在交叉免疫效应。如果这款疫苗哪怕只具有部分保护作用,并能够预防重症病例,我们立刻就拥有了一种有效武器,”莱斯屈尔说。 矿产资源开发、流离失所与战争创伤 疫情主要集中在伊图里(Ituri)矿区爆发,这绝非偶然。在当地大量非正规矿场中,数十万矿工在极其恶劣的卫生条件下工作。许多矿区位于热带雨林深处,工人们缺乏食物来源,只能依靠野生动物肉类维生,而这些动物正是病毒的主要传播媒介。同时,矿区和流民营地人口高度密集,也为病毒传播创造了理想条件。 此外,当地民众还承受着沉重的心理创伤。病毒袭击的是一个饱受战争折磨长达三十年的地区。“我们接触到许多家庭,他们因亲人在逃难途中被杀害而遭受严重创伤,”恩加乔莱在布尼亚(Bunia)接受瑞士资讯采访时表示。 在这样的背景下,任何一例因埃博拉而死亡的病例都会带来加倍的打击。由于存在传染风险,传统守灵和遗体告别仪式被禁止。死者必须立即在严格防护措施下下葬。此外,患者家中的衣物、床垫以及生活垃圾等物品都必须焚烧处理。对于原本就一贫如洗的家庭而言,这意味着失去仅有的生活财产。所以,Medair也会向死去亲人的家庭提供物资援助,以帮助他们重新开始生活。 在刚果民主共和国东部,许多家庭在矿山极其恶劣的卫生条件下,为全球科技企业工作。 Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. All Rights Reserved. 数字化繁荣背后的代价 在恩加乔莱看来,抗击埃博拉不能仅仅停留在医疗紧急援助层面。“我们需要推动结构性变革,而且必须覆盖整条生产链,”这位医生强调,只有这样,才能从根本上改善矿区灾难性的卫生条件。 因此,这场疫情也是全球消费模式的一面镜子。莱斯屈尔说:“刚果开采的矿产被用于制造我们的智能手机、电脑以及许多日常电子设备。而在这些产品背后,隐藏着严重的连锁后果:对自然资源的掠夺性开发、生态系统的破坏、地缘政治紧张局势,以及当地民众所承受的苦难。” 莱斯屈尔表示,疫情从来不只是单纯的自然现象,而往往也是经济和社会选择的结果。“我们的消费习惯会在全球范围内产生影响。” 瑞士参与刚果民主共和国埃博拉防控行动 2026年,瑞士将其用于支持刚果民主共和国(DRK)抗击埃博拉疫情的援助资金提高至460万瑞郎(约合3740万人民币)。这些资金主要用于支持世界卫生组织(WHO)、协调医疗应急队伍、提升实验室检测能力以及加强流行病学监测。 据瑞士联邦外交部(EDA)介绍,瑞士还与刚果卫生部门及国际合作伙伴保持密切协作。 瑞士还支持多个援助组织,其中包括Medair,在疫情最严重的伊图里(Ituri)和北基伍(Nord-Kivu)两省开展项目。 此外,瑞士科研机构与刚果合作伙伴共同参与病毒基因组监测工作。总部位于巴塞尔的制药企业罗氏(Roche)提供实验室设备,并与刚果国家公共卫生研究所(INSP)合作测试本迪布焦型(Bundibugyo)埃博拉病毒的检测方法。 瑞士民众的捐款也被用于抗击埃博拉疫情。2026年5月,慈善组织幸运链(Glückskette)从紧急援助基金中拨出120万瑞郎,用于支持相关防疫工作。 https://www.swissinfo.ch/chi/swi/92226344

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
